# Skills · G · part 21

822 entries, gene-loading-z-score-standardization to general-pptx. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **gene-loading-z-score-standardization**: Use when you have gene loading (weight) vectors from an ICA decomposition of log-transformed tumor scRNA-seq expression and need comparable loading scales across components before downstream clustering or meta-component mapping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-loading-z-score-standardization))
- **gene-location-file-preparation**: Use when running the scWizard 'inferCNV' module on an annotated cancer scRNA-seq dataset (e.g., the ICC case study) and a gene-to-chromosomal-coordinate mapping file must be prepared before copy number analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-location-file-preparation))
- **gene-locus-isoform-filtering**: Use when comparing de novo transcriptome assemblies against a reference annotation and you need per-locus counts of uniquely assigned isoforms, especially for high-isoform-diversity genes like ATP6AP2. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-locus-isoform-filtering))
- **gene-lookup**: Look up gene or protein information from biological database IDs and accessions. Use when working with gene IDs, protein accessions, or identifiers from UniProt, Ensembl, FlyBase, WormBase, NCBI/RefSeq, or similar databases. Covers: identifying what database an ID comes from, converting IDs to gene symbols or names, retrieving protein function or annotation, batch querying APIs, and cross-referencing between databases. Use whenever someone asks "what gene is this", "look up this protein", "get info on these accessions", or needs to map between identifier systems. Also use for phylogenetic tre… ([MusserLab/lab-claude-skills](https://github.com/MusserLab/lab-claude-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/genome-annotation/skills/gene-lookup) · ★6 · 2 copies)
- **gene-mapping-variance-quantification**: Use when a trained noise-augmented GAN mapping between transcriptomic and proteomic domains is available and you need to quantify which genes' generated values are stable vs. unstable under stochastic z draws. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-mapping-variance-quantification))
- **gene-marker-selection**: Use when you have a scRNA-seq expression matrix with categorical cell labels (flat or hierarchical) and must design a minimal gene panel that discriminates all labels jointly with high precision. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-marker-selection--c8a860b8c61d2542))
- **gene-marker-selection**: Use when scRNA-seq data contains many phenotype groups (e.g., cell-type/tissue combinations) with overlapping DE genes and a single global marker set is needed that distinguishes every phenotype pair, not just markers per individual cell type. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-marker-selection--65298a8bc79efe95))
- **gene-mean-variance-curve-fitting**: Use when identifying overdispersed/highly-variable features from normalized count or intensity matrices across modalities (bulk RNA-Seq, proteomics, snRNA-Seq) where a modality-specific Poisson model does not apply. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-mean-variance-curve-fitting))
- **gene-median-expression-filtering**: Use when a raw TPM expression matrix (e.g. from the Soybean Expression Atlas plus supplementary infection RNA-seq data) must be filtered to remove lowly expressed genes before building a coexpression network. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-median-expression-filtering))
- **gene-membership-table-matching**: Use when two WGCNA/PyWGCNA networks built from different datasets (e.g., 5xFAD and 3xTg-AD MODEL-AD mouse RNA-seq) must be compared module-by-module to find conserved co-expression signatures via significant gene-set overlap. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-membership-table-matching))
- **gene-metabolite-annotation-mapping**: Use when an XCMS-style feature intensity matrix must be tied to gene function: exact-mass matching of m/z features to HMDB metabolites, removing unannotated features, and testing DAM enrichment among gene-associated metabolite sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-annotation-mapping))
- **gene-metabolite-association-mining**: Use when a pseudotargeted metabolomics dataset over a mutant/genotype panel needs to be mined for metabolite-mutation associations, e.g. ranking mutants by module eigenmetabolite to find genes controlling a specific metabolite like glucoraphanin. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-association-mining))
- **gene-metabolite-colocalization-analysis**: Use when haCCA-integrated Data objects (aligned MALDI-MSI features on 10X Visium spots) are available and the goal is to find genes whose spatial pattern co-localizes with specific metabolites. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-colocalization-analysis))
- **gene-metabolite-enrichment-testing**: Use when you have a patient-vs-control metabolite z-score matrix and need to convert DAMs into per-gene enrichment scores via gene-metabolite associations before ranking candidate genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-enrichment-testing))
- **gene-metabolite-ora-vs-espia-comparison**: Use when you have both gene and metabolite feature tables mapped to KEGG pathway nodes and need to compare topology-aware impact analysis (eSPIA) against ORA, or to reproduce SMART 2.0's IOPA breast cancer result (eSPIA: 2 significant pathways vs ORA: 51 at pFDR &lt; 0.05). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-ora-vs-espia-comparison))
- **gene-metabolite-pathway-membership-mapping**: Use when an analyte list contains both genes/proteins and metabolites and you need their pathway memberships consolidated from multiple pathway databases before enrichment or clustering analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-pathway-membership-mapping))
- **gene-metabolite-pearson-correlation**: Use when integrating spatial transcriptome and MALDI-MSI data at spot resolution and you need to select gene–metabolite feature pairs whose Pearson correlation on anchor spot pairs will drive CCA-based feature enrichment before ICP alignment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-metabolite-pearson-correlation))
- **gene-metric-bin-assignment**: Use when you have a per-gene metric table (e.g. Tajima's D or nucleotide diversity per gene) and a nematode/zebrafish scRNA count object (AnnData via Scanpy), and want a binned gene-category-weighted expression index instead of a gene-age-weighted one. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-metric-bin-assignment))
- **gene-model-reference-file-verification**: Reference for reproducing or validating the SkewC gene-model filtering step when the default hg38/mm10 .norRNAtRNA.bed references cannot be used, e.g. a new assembly or GENCODE release. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-model-reference-file-verification))
- **gene-module-activity-inference**: Use when you have a single-cell count matrix (AnnData/scanpy) plus a GMT gene-module file and need per-cell, interpretable activity scores for pathways or marker sets, including out-of-sample inference on unseen cells or studies. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-activity-inference))
- **gene-module-activity-inference-single-cell**: Use when you have a preprocessed single-cell dataset (e.g., Scanpy AnnData) and a GMT gene-set file and need pathway-level activity per cell, with downstream testing of which modules are significantly activated. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-activity-inference-single-cell))
- **gene-module-annotation-count-audit**: Use when you have the E.PATH GitHub data files (database and annotations) and need to verify or recompute the reported dataset, gene-module, gene-count, and per-environmental-factor statistics before downstream enrichment work. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-annotation-count-audit))
- **gene-module-detection**: Use when the input is a preprocessed scRNA-seq expression matrix (rows = cells, columns = genes) with cell metadata and the goal is to infer gene co-expression networks and detect trait-associated gene modules. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-detection))
- **gene-module-interpretation-for-cell-type-annotation**: Use when you have run JOINTLY and need to interpret its learned factors as gene modules — e.g., to annotate cell types, confirm marker genes (DCN for IMR90, CD3B for Jurkat), or discover active biological processes per cluster. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-interpretation-for-cell-type-annotation))
- **gene-module-membership-outlier-exemption**: Use when applying leave-one-out outlier detection (L1OutExpOut) to gene sets and needing to prevent over-removal of genes, e.g. genes flagged as outliers in many collections or belonging to only a few gene sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-membership-outlier-exemption))
- **gene-module-variational-autoencoder-training**: Use when a single-cell RNA-seq dataset (AnnData/scanpy) must be embedded in a biologically interpretable latent space defined by prior gene annotations (MSigDB, Reactome, GO, cell type markers), e.g. for pathway activity or drug-response interpretation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-module-variational-autoencoder-training))
- **gene-name-alias-mapping**: Use when refining an inferred GRN against curated databases (OmniPath, Signor, SignaLink, TRRUST) and gene/organism labels from a GenBank file may miss synonyms, causing incomplete interaction retrieval. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-name-alias-mapping))
- **gene-network-benchmarking-metrics**: Use when comparing gene network inference methods or model variants against a curated directed binary ground truth (e.g. Omnipath) and reporting AUPRC and EPR per cell type or condition. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-network-benchmarking-metrics))
- **gene-network-centrality-analysis**: Use when you have weighted, cell-type-specific gene network adjacency matrices (e.g., GRnnData objects from scPRINT) and need to identify differential hub genes or functionally enriched network communities, as done for the BPH fibroblast ion-exchange/senescence module. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-network-centrality-analysis))
- **gene-network-heads-selection**: Use when extracting a gene regulatory network from a pretrained transformer's attention matrices and you must decide which attention heads to average, using a literature ground truth such as Omnipath. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-network-heads-selection))
- **gene-network-inference-from-attention-matrices**: Use when inferring genome-wide gene regulatory networks from a single-cell transformer model's attention matrices and benchmarking against ChIP-seq/perturb-seq gold-standard networks with EPR and AUPRC metrics. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-network-inference-from-attention-matrices))
- **gene-network-scoring-analysis**: Use when you have a data frame (CSV format) of significantly changed genes with log-fold-change values from single-cell RNA-seq or proteomics experiments and need per-gene LFC scores for GMM-based gene-network exploration in OmicInt. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-network-scoring-analysis))
- **gene-network-seed-initialization**: Use when when you have metabolomic enrichment scores for genes (e.g., from MSEA analysis comparing a patient to controls) and need to prepare them as seed labels for label propagation over a gene/protein interaction network. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/gene-network-seed-initialization))
- **gene-neuron-correspondence-mapping**: Use when reconstructing or retraining DCNet: you need to wire the 9078x434 zero-one gene-cell matrix into the network's input-to-hidden weights and align input/output neuron ordering with the gene index. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-neuron-correspondence-mapping))
- **gene-nextflow**: Build, run, and debug Nextflow data pipelines and nf-core workflows for genomics. Use when the user mentions Nextflow, nf-core, .nf files, nextflow.config, DSL2, or wants to run community pipelines (nf-core/rnaseq, nf-core/sarek for variant calling), configure executors/containers (Docker, Singularity/Apptainer, Conda, Wave), or scale to HPC/SLURM or cloud. Triggers include 'Nextflow', 'nf-core', 'nextflow.config', 'DSL2', 'samplesheet', '-resume', 'Singularity', 'Sarek', 'rnaseq pipeline'. ([oscar-wang-xin/my-skills](https://github.com/oscar-wang-xin/my-skills/tree/HEAD/gene-nextflow))
- **gene-ontology**: Apply Gene Ontology in computational biology and life sciences. Use when analyzing biological data, developing therapeutics, or building bioinformatics pipelines. This skill covers methodologies, tools, and applications in gene ontology. ([Sandeeprdy1729/skill_galaxy](https://github.com/Sandeeprdy1729/skill_galaxy/tree/HEAD/skills/bio/gene-ontology) · ★1)
- **gene-ontology-annotation-mapping**: Use when you have mouse gene clusters/modules (e.g., co-expression GEPs) and need to determine which GO or MP terms are statistically enriched, with enrichment ratios and BH-adjusted p-values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-annotation-mapping))
- **gene-ontology-annotation-mapping-mouse**: Use when mouse (3T3-L1) differential expression results need to be translated into GO terms — e.g. ranking genes by log fold-change and testing which adipogenic GO categories (BP/CC/MF) are significantly enriched. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-annotation-mapping-mouse))
- **gene-ontology-annotation-similarity-computation**: Use when you need to assess the functional coherence of protein sequence clusters (e.g., produced by Linclust, UCLUST, CD-HIT) via GO or Pfam annotation agreement. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-ontology-annotation-similarity-computation))
- **gene-ontology-annotation-via-biomart**: Use when a pre-computed cell-cell interaction (CCI) dataset of enriched int-pairs must be annotated with Gene Ontology functional terms, as a prerequisite for ranking terms by annotated int-pair occurrence. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-annotation-via-biomart))
- **gene-ontology-biological-process-enrichment**: Use when you have a ranked or significant gene list (e.g., SigGenes at fdr level 0.01) and need to confirm it is biologically meaningful via GO biological-process term enrichment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-biological-process-enrichment))
- **gene-ontology-chord-plot-visualization**: Use when the input is a GO analysis result table (e.g. chord_example.csv) linking genes to GO terms and the goal is a circos-style chord diagram rendered without programming, via GraphBio's chord plot module. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-ontology-chord-plot-visualization))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-along-trajectory**: Use when you have LatentVelo cell trajectories (z_traj, times from ltv.cell_trajectories) for a lineage such as beta cell differentiation and need biological validation of the trajectory via enriched GO terms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-along-trajectory))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have a BIT output rank table (e.g., mm10_RUNX1_rank_table.csv) of TRs ranked by BIT score from perturbation, cancer-type, or cell-type-specific epigenomic query regions and need to test GO BP enrichment of the top candidates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis--b056e50d15cd61ba))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have per-cluster up-/down-regulated gene lists from one-vs-rest SAM differential expression and need GO terms explaining the biology of each cluster. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis--226a2d138a89352a))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have gene-to-module assignments from co-expression clustering (e.g., SGCRNA modules from Julia's SpectralClustering, or WGCNA modules such as brown4/grey) and need to test which GO terms are over-represented in each module to validate or compare module biological identity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when comparing functional enrichment of subpopulation gene sets derived from scRNA-seq alone versus scRNA-seq combined with bulk HiChIP loop data, to quantify whether loop information improves GO enrichment (Table 2). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis--bd4377685ab08d82))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have cluster labels from single-cell RNA-seq clustering (e.g., scEFSC wMetaC output) plus a normalized expression matrix, and need to interpret clusters biologically via GO/KEGG enrichment of top DE genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis--a98045df37f218fd))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have a small detected gene set (e.g., EDGE feature genes below the μ − 1.5 × σ entropy cutoff) and need to test whether it is enriched for GO biological processes relative to an appropriate background universe. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis--fa53d4a47acc6eca))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have a gene list derived from differential IPA usage (or another differential transcript event) and want to identify enriched GO biological-process terms explaining which functions the affected genes share. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis--a3440c218bc17fc3))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis-via-toppfun**: Use when a subclone-associated gene list (e.g. GATA2R361C subclone genes) overlaps a co-expression module (e.g. TCGA VIM-correlated genes, 2191 genes at q &lt; 0.001) and the intersecting genes need biological interpretation via GO terms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-analysis-via-toppfun))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-comparison**: Use when scCRISPR-seq analysis needs to judge whether decoupled perturbation responses (vs the naive OC estimate) yield better pathway enrichment and perturbation-related gene rankings, e.g. for TCR-related genes like CD69 and RELA on Jurkat CROP-seq data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-comparison))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-interpretation**: Use when you have a ranked list of genes from EpiAlign differential alignment (Wilcoxon P-values comparing within-tissue vs cross-tissue alignment scores) and need functional interpretation via GO terms or validation against known tissue-specific gene labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-interpretation))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-of-sub-compartments**: Use when genes have been assigned to CscoreTool-M sub-compartments (e.g., C10_2 or C10_1 from a 10-sub-compartment run on Hi-C data) and you need to test whether these sub-compartments are enriched for housekeeping or cell-type-specific biological functions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-of-sub-compartments))
- **gene-ontology-enrichment-visualization**: Use when you have completed a GO enrichment analysis (e.g., via hypergeometric test or similar) and need to visualize the results as a dotplot. Input is a CSV table with columns for GO term identifiers, p-values or adjusted p-values, gene ratios (or counts), and gene count; ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-enrichment-visualization))
- **gene-ontology-gaf-annotation-parsing**: Use when DE summary statistics must be paired with pre-defined gene set annotations (e.g. for iDEA GSE input) and no annotation matrix matching the required gene identifier type exists yet. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-gaf-annotation-parsing))
- **gene-ontology-geneset-scoring**: Use when you have scRNA-seq data with known cell-type labels across datasets and want to test whether specific functional gene sets (e.g., GO terms) carry enough cell-type signal to recover labels via cross-dataset neighbor voting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-geneset-scoring))
- **gene-ontology-overrepresentation-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have a list of significant DE gene identifiers (e.g. deseq2_de_genes.txt) from simulated RNA-seq data and need GO Biological Process, Molecular Function and Cellular Component over-representation results. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-ontology-overrepresentation-enrichment-analysis))
- **gene-ontology-pathway-annotation-mapping**: Use when assigning functional titles to gene-set or module gene lists by consolidating enrichment evidence (GOTERM, KEGG, BioCarta, GSAn, RcisTarget motifs) across all 382 BloodGen3 modules. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-pathway-annotation-mapping))
- **gene-ontology-pathway-enrichment**: Use when you have a set of small RNAs (e.g., common tRF-5/tRF-3 tncRNAs, 18-25 nt) whose predicted protein-coding mRNA targets need pathway/GO enrichment analysis to infer biological function. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/gene-ontology-pathway-enrichment))
- **gene-ontology-pathway-enrichment-analysis**: Use when a list of predicted mRNA/protein targets of small RNAs (e.g. tRF-5 and tRF-3 series tncRNAs of 18-25 nt) is available and you need to identify overrepresented biological processes such as metabolic and developmental pathways. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-pathway-enrichment-analysis--51fda91503249e46))
- **gene-ontology-pathway-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have a ranked or top-N gene list per class/state (e.g. top 20 DWGs from Pollock feature attribution) and need to identify enriched GO pathways and verify biological plausibility. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-pathway-enrichment-analysis--5c043a3ae776605d))
- **gene-ontology-similarity-network-construction**: Use when preparing the gene universe for RNA differential expression simulation: you need protein-coding genes on a target chromosome (e.g. chr22) that share sufficient GO-term content, with a simthreshold (default 0.3) defining edge retention. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-ontology-similarity-network-construction))
- **gene-ontology-term-overlap-scoring**: Use when evaluating whether an integrative clustering (e.g. KLIC vs COCA) better exploits the biological information in multiple omic datasets, by scoring cluster coherence through shared Gene Ontology terms across BP, MF and CC ontologies. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-ontology-term-overlap-scoring))
- **gene-ontology-term-overrepresentation**: Use when you have a gene list from differential expression (e.g. consensus DEGs selected by a minimum vote threshold) and need overrepresentation of biological processes such as basement membrane or ECM terms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ontology-term-overrepresentation))
- **gene-overlap-across-sample-pairs**: Use when you have ranked candidate reference gene lists from ERgene FindERG over multiple sample pairs of one dataset (e.g., 20 genes per pair) and need to quantify which genes recur across pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-overlap-across-sample-pairs))
- **gene-overlap-enrichment-analysis**: Use when you have two gene/region sets (e.g., differentially modified regions from ChIP-seq and differentially expressed genes from RNA-seq) mapped to a common identifier and need to quantify overlap significance and GO enrichment. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-overlap-enrichment-analysis))
- **gene-pair-deletion-screening**: Use when you have mock and infected context-specific genome-scale metabolic models (e.g., built from GSE147507/GSE148729 lung expression data) and need to find knockouts that selectively impair viral biomass while sparing host biomass. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pair-deletion-screening))
- **gene-pair-difference-normalization**: Use when you must timestamp individual bulk or scRNAseq pseudobulk transcriptomic samples (e.g., microarray or RNAseq) whose circadian phase is unknown and no matched control series exists for cross-sample normalization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pair-difference-normalization))
- **gene-pair-feature-selection**: Use when building an ICB response prediction model from RNA-seq expression/gene-pair matrices and you need batch-robust feature reduction (e.g., hundreds of gene pairs down to a top-5 support set) prior to ensemble learning. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-pair-feature-selection))
- **gene-pair-prognostic-association-analysis**: Use when bulk RNA-seq/microarray with matched survival data must be tested for whether immune cell types or immune marker genes appear spuriously favorable due to co-infiltration, as in TCGA SKCM06 melanoma. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pair-prognostic-association-analysis))
- **gene-pair-score-computation**: Use when a bulk transcriptomic cohort has been preprocessed with marker-gene selection and you need a pairwise logical-relational matrix of marker expression comparisons to feed Cox regression or enrichment of cell-cell interactions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pair-score-computation))
- **gene-pair-specificity-evaluation**: Use when a set of candidate ligand-receptor pairs has been inferred between two cell types and you must determine whether each pair is restricted to that sender-receiver combination or also appears in other cell-type interactions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pair-specificity-evaluation))
- **gene-pair-to-drug-combination-mapping**: Use when you have a list of essential genes and gene pairs (from single-/double-gene knockout analysis, e.g. FBA with a VBOF) and need to convert them into approved single-drug and drug-combination candidates via DrugBank target mapping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pair-to-drug-combination-mapping))
- **gene-pairwise-correlation-analysis**: Use when you have a log2-transformed expression matrix (data frame) of multiple genes/features (e.g. 40 genes across ~10K TCGA Pan-Cancer samples) and need a pairwise correlation heatmap ordered by hierarchical clustering into feature clusters. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-pairwise-correlation-analysis))
- **gene-panel-design-agent**: --&gt; ([FreedomIntelligence/OpenClaw-Medical-Skills](https://github.com/FreedomIntelligence/OpenClaw-Medical-Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/gene-panel-design-agent) · ★3.1k · 12 copies)
- **gene-panel-design-agent**: AI-powered design of targeted gene panels for clinical and research applications including cancer diagnostics, pharmacogenomics, and rare disease testing. ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-panel-design-agent) · ★104 · 4 copies)
- **gene-panel-intersection-across-datasets**: Use when a query scRNA-seq AnnData has a different gene count or ordering than the SIMS TabNet reference model's training features, and cell-type label prediction must proceed without feature-mismatch errors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-panel-intersection-across-datasets))
- **gene-panel-membership-tallying**: Use when a CPSR/PCGR virtual gene panel or superpanel gene-definition file must be decomposed into its contributing gene sources (CPG_SOURCE categories) and counted, e.g. to report panel composition as of a given data release date. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/gene-panel-membership-tallying))
- **gene-panel-tissue-optimality-analysis**: Decision support for choosing the RNA-seq biosample for a disease gene panel: you have per-tissue MRSD scores (*.results.mrsd.txt) and a panel gene list, and need the tissue minimizing required sequencing depth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-panel-tissue-optimality-analysis))
- **gene-pathomechanism-interpretation**: Use when patient SVs (inversions, translocations, deletions, duplications) in hg19 coordinates need candidate causative genes and coding or long-range pathomechanisms assigned in a phenotype-specific cellular context. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-pathomechanism-interpretation))
- **gene-pathway-network-extension**: Use when you have a list of differentially expressed genes (e.g., selected by an absolute log2 fold change cutoff such as &gt; 1) and want to annotate/link them to curated pathway resources (WikiPathways) as a network extension in Cytoscape. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-pathway-network-extension))
- **gene-peak-pair-association-modeling**: Use when you have a SnapATAC snap object for scATAC-seq cells plus matched scRNA-seq expression and need to connect distal accessible peaks to putative target genes, e.g. building a cis-regulatory network from the 10X_PBMC_15K PBMC dataset. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-peak-pair-association-modeling))
- **gene-periodicity-magnitude-quantification**: Use when you have Cyclum-inferred circular pseudo-time for cells in two or more conditions (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-periodicity-magnitude-quantification))
- **gene-phenotype-annotation-mapping**: Use when a candidate gene must be scored for its association with a structural-variant-associated phenotype group (head_neck, limbs, neurodevelopmental, cardiovascular) using HPO/MPO gene-phenotype annotations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-phenotype-annotation-mapping))
- **gene-prediction-accuracy-evaluation**: Use when you need to quantify and compare gene prediction accuracy across gene finders on metagenome assemblies, using a reference annotation to count missed genes, start-site errors, or nucleotide Sn/Sp. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-prediction-accuracy-evaluation--55bbbc1b859c47fc))
- **gene-prediction-accuracy-evaluation**: Use when comparing gene caller output (e.g., Glimmer-MG predictions) to known gene coordinates from GenBank reference genomes or simulated metagenomes, to quantify sensitivity and precision per tool, read length, or error rate. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-prediction-accuracy-evaluation--4b3f7f67d21b6c63))
- **gene-prediction-error-counting**: Use when benchmarking a new gene prediction tool's start-codon accuracy against reference GeneMarkS-2 annotations and you need per-group error counts split by regulatory signal class (canonical RBS, non-canonical RBS, leaderless). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-prediction-error-counting))
- **gene-prediction-model-selection**: Use when annotating assembled human microbiome metagenome contigs and you must choose gene prediction parameters per contig, assigning contigs to species-level pan-genomes or routing unmatched contigs to fallback algorithms. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-prediction-model-selection))
- **gene-prediction-open-reading-frame-calling**: Use when assembled contigs or long-read sequences must be annotated with predicted ORFs before exclusion-based viral classification, e.g., after SPAdes/metaSPAdes/MEGAHIT assembly or when loading contigs via virMine's -A option. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-prediction-open-reading-frame-calling))
- **gene-prediction-runtime-benchmarking**: Use when comparing computational cost or database footprint of gene finders on metagenomic contigs, or when reproducing the GeneMark-HM efficiency results (12 min on 45 Mb, ~80% in BLASTn, 15x pan-genome reduction). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-prediction-runtime-benchmarking))
- **gene-presence-absence-feature-modeling**: Use when the goal is to predict microbial phenotypic traits (e.g. gram-negative status, spore formation, phototrophy) from COG presence/absence profiles, especially for incomplete metagenomic genome bins rather than finished reference genomes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-presence-absence-feature-modeling))
- **gene-presence-absence-feature-selection**: Use when building lifestyle or metadata classifiers from a binary gene cluster presence/absence matrix (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-presence-absence-feature-selection))
- **gene-presence-fraction-filtering**: Use when preparing a raw scRNA-seq/CITE-seq count matrix (e.g., an AnnData .h5ad loaded with scanpy) for marker selection, and you need to discard genes expressed in too few or too many cells before normalization and z-scaling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-presence-fraction-filtering))

- **gene-prioritization-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when reproducing or comparing phenotype-based gene prioritizer benchmark results, e.g. counting causal-gene ranks, top-20 recall, or unique top-N hit counts across VIBE, Phenomizer, AMELIE, GADO, PhenIX, hiPHIVE, PhenoTips and PubCaseFinder. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/gene-prioritization-benchmark-evaluation))
- **gene-prioritization-benchmarking**: Use when a gene prioritization method must be quantitatively validated against known causative genes — via permutation tests, percentile-of-causative-gene metrics, or ablations of the underlying interaction network. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-prioritization-benchmarking))
- **gene-prioritization-deep-learning**: Use when you have non-coding variants already scored by a brain-variant prioritizer (ncDeepBrain) and need to assign per-gene susceptibility probabilities by linking variants to genes and training a deep network on gene-level features. - **gene-prioritization-ranking-by-gda-score**: Use when HPO terms (or other phenotype concepts) have already been mapped to UMLS CUIs and you need to retrieve matching DisGeNET GDAs from a triple store and output a gene list ranked by highest GDA score. - **gene-profile**: Build a researcher-first, citation-checked profile of a human gene — what it does and how we know, protein structure and variants (allelic series), expression, human genetics, disease links, research toolkit (tool compounds, models, cell lines), what is established, contested and open, a literature reading path, then druggability, clinical and safety context — as &lt;GENE&gt;-profile.md with figures (PDF on request), optionally scoped to one disease. - **gene-prognosis-scan**: Pan-cancer prognosis scan for gene lists. Use when the user wants to scan genes for cancer survival/prognosis, correlate a gene with immune infiltration, or map mouse genes to human orthologs. ([PanCodeInventory/pi-scientist](https://github.com/PanCodeInventory/pi-scientist/tree/HEAD/skills/gene-prognosis-scan) · ★1)
- **gene-program-discovery-network-analysis**: Use when the goal is unsupervised discovery of gene programs/modules from normalized scRNA-seq data, especially when benchmarking against ICA/NMF or seeking GO-term/PPI-enriched gene modules. - **gene-protein-correspondence-mapping**: Use when constructing an intercellular communication database from protein-centric PPI sources (e.g., STRING) that must be expressed as gene pairs to match scRNA-seq gene expression matrices for human or mouse. - **gene-protein-quality**: Report gene and protein annotation quality per organism strain. Shows coverage of proteins, functional descriptions, GO terms, KEGG, eggNOG, and expression data. - **gene-protein-reaction-association-assembly**: Use when building a draft genome-scale metabolic model from annotated amino-acid FASTA (Type 1) or DIAMOND BLASTp hits (Type 2) and reactions must carry gene-reaction provenance from KEGG annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-protein-reaction-association-assembly))
- **gene-protein-reaction-association-evaluation**: Use when you have a constraint-based metabolic model with embedded GPR rules (AND/OR associations between genes and reactions), paired with quantified gene-expression data (RNA-seq FPKM or read counts per cell line). - **gene-protein-reaction-coverage-assessment**: Use when a genome-scale metabolic model (e.g. an SBML file such as BioModels MODEL2007210001) must be quality-controlled before or after publication, and GPR coverage, metabolite annotation, or an overall MEMOTE score are needed as evidence of community-standard compliance. - **gene-pysam**: Python/HTSlib workflows for genomic files. Use when reading, querying, filtering, or writing SAM/BAM/CRAM, VCF/BCF, FASTA/FASTQ, or tabix data with pysam — including pileup, coverage, indexing, CRAM references, and variant extraction. Triggers include 'pysam', 'BAM', 'CRAM', 'VCF', 'samtools', 'bcftools', 'pileup', 'coverage', 'AlignmentFile', 'VariantFile'. - **gene-rank-frequency-quantification**: Use when benchmarking phenotype-based gene prioritization tools and you need to detect systematic bias, e.g. certain genes appearing disproportionately often in top-10/top-20 ranked output positions across many patient cases. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/gene-rank-frequency-quantification))
- **gene-rank-interpretation**: Use when a seed-gene set (e.g. jasmonate genes AT2G44810 and AT1G17420) has been run through RWR_LOE or RWR_CV on a multiplex and you must judge rank plausibility, functional enrichment, or extract/visualize the top-ranked neighborhood. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-rank-interpretation))
- **gene-ranking-and-top-k-selection**: Use when a labeled single-cell training matrix with cell type annotations needs supervised feature selection before building SciBet (or similar multinomial) classifiers. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ranking-and-top-k-selection))
- **gene-ranking-comparison-across-tools**: Use when a set of SVs with known disease-causative genes (positive controls) must be scored by multiple pathogenicity classifiers and ranked-gene agreement between tools (e.g. POSTRE vs TADEUS2) quantified. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-ranking-comparison-across-tools))
- **gene-ranking-matrix-construction**: Use when a normalized UMI matrix from scRNA-seq data (e.g., SingCellaR Human_HSPC) must be converted into AUCell rankings to score lineage signature gene sets loaded from a GMT file. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-ranking-matrix-construction))
- **gene-recall-curve-analysis**: Use when you have per-cell-type ranked biomarker lists (e.g. top-100 from scaLR ClasswiseAbs) and a curated cell-type-specific reference marker database, and need to quantify/plot how many validated markers each tool recovers. - **gene-region-alignment-score-ranking**: Use when a query gene's chromatin state sequence must be compared against every protein-coding gene region in one Roadmap/ChromHMM-annotated sample to produce a ranked local-alignment score table identifying the -matching gene region. - **gene-regulation-count-annotation**: Use when you have a precomputed DE table (log2fc, negLog10p, SampleType, timePoint) or matrix+metadata and need faceted volcano plots with per-panel up/down/neutral gene counts annotated. - **gene-regulator-correlation-null-comparison**: Use when validating a multi-layer GRN inference method against simulated ground truth: MOSim supplies both the count matrices (RNA-seq, miRNA-seq, TF) and the active regulatory relationships needed as reference edges and as a correlation null. - **gene-regulator-correlation-validation**: Use when you have simulated or inferred multi-omics data with a ground-truth list of regulator–gene interactions and need to confirm that modelled regulatory effects produce detectable correlations distinct from a no-effect background. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulator-correlation-validation))
- **gene-regulatory-network-backbone-design**: Use when simulating single-cell dynamics with dyngen and the user must define or choose a module network backbone that drives the topology (e.g. bifurcation, loop) of the resulting gene regulatory network. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-backbone-design))
- **gene-regulatory-network-centrality-analysis**: Use when cluster-specific GRN Links objects are available and you need to rank TFs by network position (degree/eigenvector centrality) or classify network configuration (cartography) across a reprogramming trajectory. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-centrality-analysis))
- **gene-regulatory-network-construction**: Use when analyzing full-scale or small-scale CROP-seq/Perturb-seq CRISPRi screens with TSS-repression and trans-association tables from Normalisr, and the goal is a causal gRNA-&gt;target-&gt;trans-gene regulatory network reproducible across gRNAs and screens. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-construction))
- **gene-regulatory-network-edge-pruning**: Use when a base GRN built from single-cell epigenomic data (e.g., Mouse sci-ATAC-seq Atlas peaks) must be converted into cluster-specific, weighted GRNs using a matched scRNA-seq expression matrix with defined cell clusters. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-edge-pruning))
- **gene-regulatory-network-edge-ranking**: Use when an overall precision-recall evaluation of a GRN inference method (e.g., SINGE Borda-aggregated edge ranks) looks acceptable but you need to check whether performance is driven or hidden by the prevalence of individual regulators in the gold standard. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-edge-ranking))
- **gene-regulatory-network-edge-recovery-evaluation**: Use when a reconstructed GRN (directed signed edges/weights) must be scored against a known ground-truth network — e.g., oracle-case simulations with known true mean and covariance matrices — to quantify edge recovery via precision and recall. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-edge-recovery-evaluation))
- **gene-regulatory-network-ensemble-evaluation**: Use when you have a full synthetic/simulated groundtruth network plus expression data (HDF5/CSV/EXP) and need to test whether ensemble GRN coefficients remain reliable when optimized on only 0.5%-10% of the truth. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-ensemble-evaluation))
- **gene-regulatory-network-ground-truth-validation**: Use when a new or reimplemented fate-inference or TF-activity method needs validation against a synthetic dataset with a known ground-truth GRN before application to real scRNA-seq data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-ground-truth-validation))
- **gene-regulatory-network-inference**: Use when scRNA-seq (optionally with scATAC-seq/motif hints) must be turned into a cell-type-specific directed TF-gene network whose target genes are explained by Boolean logic combinations of &lt;5 candidate regulators. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-inference--34e37e91495b0533))
- **gene-regulatory-network-inference**: Use when you have pseudotime-ordered single-cell expression data (a .mat file with X, ptime, gene_list, optional regix/branches) and need a ranked list of directed regulator-gene interactions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-inference--9a4cb1a7cfd41423))
- **gene-regulatory-network-inference**: Use when a raw or preprocessed scRNA-seq reference dataset needs a TF-gene regulatory graph to guide causal GAN training (GRouNdGAN) or to serve as ground truth for benchmarking GRN inference methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-inference--3c087b5f4c47dde4))
- **gene-regulatory-network-inference-from-pseudotemporal-single-cell-data**: Use when input is pseudotemporal single-cell gene expression (a .mat file with sparse matrix X, ptime array, optional regix regulator indices) and the goal is a ranked list of transcriptional regulator-target interactions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-inference-from-pseudotemporal-single-cell-data))
- **gene-regulatory-network-inference-from-scrnaseq**: Use when a pre-inferred co-expression module (GCN module, e.g. WGCNA/BioNERO-derived gene lists) must be converted into a directed, motif-validated GRN to identify its key transcription-factor regulators. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-inference-from-scrnaseq))
- **gene-regulatory-network-inference-via-expectation-maximization**: Use when single-cell transcriptomics data (scRNA-seq expression matrix with cluster labels) requires joint inference of a gene regulatory network and per-cell RNA velocities, especially to resolve directional dynamics like state transdifferentiation that standard methods miss. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-inference-via-expectation-maximization))
- **gene-regulatory-network-informed-decoder-wiring**: Use when you have single-cell transcriptomics plus a prior gene regulatory network (e.g. ARACNe-derived TF-target modules) and want an interpretable latent space whose dimensions correspond to transcription factor / pathway activity rather than abstract factors. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-informed-decoder-wiring))
- **gene-regulatory-network-method-comparison**: Use when validating a gene regulatory network inference method on time-course expression data against a curated reference regulon set, especially to compare two methods and a random baseline per transcription factor. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-method-comparison))
- **gene-regulatory-network-module-partitioning**: Use when you have a merged, edge-pruned GRN from single-cell or spatial transcriptomics data (e.g., snRNAseq or Visium) and need to identify gene modules, compute per-cell/spot module scores, and link modules to traits or pathways. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-module-partitioning))
- **gene-regulatory-network-neighborhood-extraction**: Use when you have an inferred lineage-specific GRN plus driver regulators with influence scores and need gene modules around those regulators with per-cell activity scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-neighborhood-extraction))
- **gene-regulatory-network-perturbation-scoring**: Use when untreated scRNA-seq data from diseased tissue has a known drug target (e.g. Brd4 for BET inhibitors) and you must rank which cell types are most drug-responsive via in silico target perturbation of GRNs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-perturbation-scoring))
- **gene-regulatory-network-prior-integration**: Use when single-cell RNA-seq count data must be embedded with prior knowledge from a TF regulatory network (e.g., DoRothEA) so latent variables correspond to TF regulon activities, e.g., to score TF activity per cell or benchmark TF perturbation inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-prior-integration))
- **gene-regulatory-network-refinement**: Use when an initial GRN inferred from RNA-Seq expression data needs refinement with TFBM predictions and curated database interactions, especially for non-model organisms lacking database coverage. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-refinement))
- **gene-regulatory-network-representation-learning**: Use when the input is a directed GRN edge list (e.g. DawnNet, KEGG, RegNetwork) plus a gene feature matrix, and the goal is to capture directed regulation information and second-order neighbor structure as embeddings. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-representation-learning))
- **gene-regulatory-network-resource-handling**: Use when a task needs a prior-knowledge GRN (DoRothEA or OmniPath) as input for TF-activity inference or perturbation benchmarking with decoupleR/decoupleRBench, and the network's sign and weight annotations must be preserved. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-resource-handling))
- **gene-regulatory-network-simulation**: Use when benchmarking trajectory inference, RNA velocity, trajectory alignment, or cell-specific network inference methods requires simulated single-cell data with completely known ground truth for GRN dynamics and cellular progression. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-simulation))
- **gene-regulatory-network-table-export**: Use when a time-series expression dataset (e.g., mouse liver regeneration GSE95135) has been mined with TSMiner and you need machine-readable tables of time-specific activators/repressors and their interacting KEGG pathways. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-table-export))
- **gene-regulatory-network-visualization**: Use when simulated or real multi-omics count matrices plus regulator-gene associations must be inspected as multi-layered GRNs with distinct activator vs. repressor edge types. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/gene-regulatory-network-visualization))
- **gene-regulatory-networks**: Workflow for regulatory network inference, regulon scoring, perturbation-aware comparison, and network visualization. ([BioTender-max/awesome-bio-agent-skills](https://github.com/BioTender-max/awesome-bio-agent-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/bioclaw_hub/gene-regulatory-networks) · ★224 · 2 copies)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-coexpression-networks**: Build weighted gene co-expression networks to identify modules of co-regulated genes, relate them to phenotypes, and find hub genes using WGCNA, hdWGCNA, MEGENA, CEMiTool, and Gaussian graphical models. Covers signed-network choice, soft-threshold selection, module preservation, and the marginal-vs-partial-correlation distinction. Use when finding co-expression modules, identifying hub genes, relating gene networks to clinical or experimental traits, or building single-cell co-expression networks. For directed TF-target inference see scenic-regulons and grn-inference; for condition rewiring s… ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-regulatory-networks-coexpression-networks) · ★104)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-coexpression-networks**: Build weighted gene co-expression networks to identify modules of co-regulated genes and relate them to phenotypes using WGCNA and CEMiTool. Detects hub genes and module-trait relationships from bulk or single-cell expression data. Use when finding co-expression modules, identifying hub genes, or relating gene networks to clinical or experimental variables. ([BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills](https://github.com/BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills/tree/HEAD/bio-skills/gene-regulatory-networks/coexpression-networks) · ★1)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-differential-networks**: Compare gene co-expression and regulatory networks between biological conditions to find rewired relationships using DiffCorr, DiffCoEx, DINGO/iDINGO, and CoDiNA. Covers the differential-connectivity-is-not-differential-expression distinction, the pairwise multiple-testing explosion, marginal vs partial (direct) rewiring, and the underpowered-rewiring failure mode. Use when comparing co-expression networks between disease vs control, treatment, or developmental stages, or finding hub genes that rewire without changing mean expression. For single-condition modules see coexpression-networks; fo… ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-regulatory-networks-differential-networks) · ★104)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-differential-networks**: Compare gene regulatory and co-expression networks between biological conditions to identify rewired regulatory relationships using DiffCorr. Detects gained, lost, and reversed gene-gene correlations between conditions. Use when comparing co-expression networks between disease vs control, treatment conditions, or developmental stages. ([BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills](https://github.com/BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills/tree/HEAD/bio-skills/gene-regulatory-networks/differential-networks) · ★1)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-grn-inference**: Infer gene regulatory networks from bulk or general expression data with mutual-information (ARACNe) and tree-ensemble (GENIE3, GRNBoost2) methods, and infer transcription-factor protein activity from regulons with VIPER and msVIPER. Covers the activity-not-edges paradigm, the undirected-association caveat, the DREAM5 wisdom-of-crowds and method-complementarity result, AUPRC-over-AUROC evaluation, and gold-standard incompleteness. Use when inferring a regulatory network from a bulk expression matrix, finding master regulators, or scoring TF activity from a signature. For single-cell motif-pru… ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-regulatory-networks-grn-inference) · ★104)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-multiomics-grn**: Build enhancer-driven gene regulatory networks (eGRNs) by integrating single-cell RNA-seq and ATAC-seq using SCENIC+, CellOracle base GRNs, Pando, FigR, DIRECT-NET, TRIPOD, and scMEGA. Covers the accessibility-defines-enhancers principle, peak-to-gene linking and its cell-composition confound, the paired-vs-unpaired decision, and TF-region-gene eRegulon triplets. Use when analyzing 10x multiome or paired/unpaired scRNA+scATAC to infer cis-regulatory GRNs. For RNA-only regulons see scenic-regulons; for in silico TF perturbation see perturbation-simulation. ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-regulatory-networks-multiomics-grn) · ★104)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-multiomics-grn**: Build enhancer-driven gene regulatory networks by integrating single-cell RNA-seq and ATAC-seq data using SCENIC+ to identify eRegulons linking transcription factors to enhancers and target genes. Use when analyzing 10x multiome or paired scRNA+scATAC data to infer cis-regulatory GRNs. ([BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills](https://github.com/BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills/tree/HEAD/bio-skills/gene-regulatory-networks/multiomics-grn) · ★1)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-perturbation-simulation**: Simulate transcription factor perturbation effects on cell state in silico with CellOracle and Dynamo, and predict transcriptional responses to genetic perturbations with GEARS, scGen, and CPA. Covers the direction-not-magnitude principle, local-linear validity, the GRN/velocity error it inherits, baseline discipline (mean and additive baselines), and the validation gap. Use when predicting TF knockout or overexpression effects, ranking driver TFs for fate transitions, or planning perturbation experiments. For GRN construction see multiomics-grn; for experimental Perturb-seq see single-cell/p… ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-regulatory-networks-perturbation-simulation) · ★104)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-perturbation-simulation**: Simulate transcription factor perturbation effects on cell state using CellOracle, which integrates GRN inference with in silico knockout and overexpression modeling. Predicts cell identity shifts and differentiation trajectory changes from TF perturbations. Use when predicting the effect of transcription factor knockouts, planning perturbation experiments, or identifying driver TFs for cell fate transitions. ([BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills](https://github.com/BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills/tree/HEAD/bio-skills/gene-regulatory-networks/perturbation-simulation) · ★1)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-scenic-regulons**: Infer transcription factor regulons from single-cell RNA-seq with pySCENIC by combining GRNBoost2 co-expression, cisTarget motif-enrichment pruning, and AUCell per-cell activity scoring. Covers the motif-pruning-as-directionality principle, regulon specificity scoring, run-to-run stability, and database/species matching. Use when identifying TF regulons, scoring TF activity per cell, finding master regulators of cell identity, or comparing regulon activity across conditions. For enhancer-driven multiomic GRNs see multiomics-grn; for bulk inference and VIPER protein-activity see grn-inference. ([swaruplab/operon](https://github.com/swaruplab/operon/tree/HEAD/protocols/gene-regulatory-networks-scenic-regulons) · ★104)
- **gene-regulatory-networks-scenic-regulons**: Infer gene regulatory networks and identify transcription factor regulons from single-cell RNA-seq data using pySCENIC. Discovers co-expression modules with GRNBoost2, prunes by cis-regulatory motif enrichment, and scores regulon activity per cell with AUCell. Use when identifying transcription factor regulons, scoring TF activity in single cells, or finding master regulators of cell identity. ([BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills](https://github.com/BaranziniLab/biorouter-skills/tree/HEAD/bio-skills/gene-regulatory-networks/scenic-regulons) · ★1)
- **gene-regulatory-ode-parameter-fitting**: Use when fitting gene-regulator ODE parameters to smoothed time-course expression profiles (e.g. SigB regulon targets) and you need to compare regularized vs. unregularized fits or enforce biologically interpretable parameter bounds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-regulatory-ode-parameter-fitting))
- **gene-resolver**: Validate and standardize gene identifiers in standardized_var.csv (Ensembl IDs, symbols, barnyard detection) and genetic perturbation targets in standardized_obs.csv (control detection, combinatorial splitting, is_control derivation, perturbation method). ([conradry/yc-hackathon](https://github.com/conradry/yc-hackathon/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/gene-resolver))
- **gene-review**: Review the Gene Ontology annotations of a gene using the ai4curation/ai-gene-review toolkit: fetch UniProt and EBI-GOA data, cache the literature, judge every existing annotation (ACCEPT / MODIFY / REMOVE / NEW ...), and write a validated GENE-ai-review.yaml. Use when a curator asks to "review", "audit", "check" or "curate" the GO annotations of a gene or gene product in any organism (e.g. "review DANRE fezf2", "audit the GO terms on human TP53"), or asks for a gene review, an annotation review, or an ai-gene-review. Works from any directory; sets up its own checkout of ai-gene-review on firs… ([geneontology/go-skills](https://github.com/geneontology/go-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/gene-review) · ★1)
- **gene-score-matrix-processing**: Use when input is scATAC-seq raw data (fragment file or bam file) or gene score matrices, and you need to build Cellcano-ready reference/target matrices with F-test-selected features before supervised celltyping. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-score-matrix-processing))
- **gene-score-vs-bin-accuracy-comparison**: Use when evaluating which scATAC-seq input representation (ArchR-derived gene scores vs. 500-bp fixed-size bin counts) yields better Cellcano celltype prediction accuracy across benchmark tasks, especially mouse brain reference-target pairs from Fig 2. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-score-vs-bin-accuracy-comparison))
- **gene-scoring-baseline-benchmarking**: Use when you need to show that genes prioritized by a new GWAS-by-scRNA-seq method are more functionally enriched than top genes from established gene-scoring baselines such as MAGMA or eQTL-based TWAS methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-scoring-baseline-benchmarking))
- **gene-scoring-per-cell-type-from-deconvolution-model**: Use when a trained SpatialDDLS deconvolution model exists and you need to know WHICH genes the network uses to predict each cell type's proportions, e.g. to recover canonical markers (Cd8/Cd8b1, Foxp3) from predicted spot compositions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-scoring-per-cell-type-from-deconvolution-model))
- **gene-selection-by-encoder-weight-variance**: Use when a raw scRNA-seq count matrix (genes x cells) must be reduced to a compact informative gene set before dimension reduction or clustering, and noise/dropout-driven genes need to be filtered by contribution to a part-based representation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-selection-by-encoder-weight-variance))
- **gene-sequence-truncation-simulation**: Use when you need to benchmark HMM-based annotation-recovery tools (e.g. KEMET with --hmm_mode onebm) against fragmented or partial gene sequences that mimic gapped metagenome-assembled genome (MAG) assemblies. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/gene-sequence-truncation-simulation))
- **gene-set-activity-scoring**: Use when projecting a scRNA-seq query onto a pre-built ProjectSVR reference model: the query must first be converted to gene set activity scores (via ComputeModuleScore with the model's stored gss.method, e.g. AUCell or UCell) before ProjectNewdata/MapQuery can predict embeddings. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-set-activity-scoring))
- **gene-set-annotation-construction-from-degenes**: Use when GWAS summary statistics must be stratified by SNP-heritability enrichment for a gene set (e.g., tissue DE genes from exercise training) and you need to convert a gene list into a partitioned/binary LDSC annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-set-annotation-construction-from-degenes))
- **gene-set-averaged-expression-scoring**: Use when you need to translate a cell-state or pathway gene signature (e.g. a MetaTiME MeC weight table) into a per-sample numeric score in bulk RNA-seq data such as TCGA, for grouping samples into high/low signature classes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-set-averaged-expression-scoring))
- **gene-set-based-cell-type-identification**: Use when the goal is to quantify cross-dataset replicability of known cell-type labels (e.g., neuron vs non-neuron across eight scRNA-seq studies) using gene sets and AUROC, rather than cluster-level unsupervised matching. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-set-based-cell-type-identification))
- **gene-set-collection-curation**: Use when reproducing pathway-level gene-expression analyses (PLAGE, GSEA) that depend on a predefined gene-set collection whose memberships must match a historical KEGG/Biocarta version. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-set-collection-curation))
- **gene-set-consistency-filtering-across-clusters**: Use when several related (e.g., subtype-level) cell clusters each yield a ranked COVID-vs-healthy biomarker list and you need the gene subset whose expression direction is consistent across all of them. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-set-consistency-filtering-across-clusters))
- **gene-set-coordination-testing**: Use when you have a normalized cell-by-gene scRNA-seq or ST matrix and a list of gene sets, and need to decide which pathways are truly coordinated among cells without clustering, before pathway scoring or binarization. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/gene-set-coordination-testing))
- **gene-set-enrichment-analysis**: 当你手握一组基因（差异表达基因、CRISPR 筛选命中、聚类标志基因、蛋白质组命中）或带打分的排序基因表，想知道哪些生物学通路/GO 条目/基因集被显著富集时使用；做 ORA 过表征分析或 GSEA 富集分析并产出带 FDR 的结果表与点图；不适用于上游差异表达计算、纯 ID 查询或单接口通路抓取；触发词：通路富集、富集分析、基因集富集、GO 富集、KEGG、Reactome、GSEA、ORA、过表征、pathway enrichment、gene set enrichment、functional annotation ([findscripter/everything-skills](https://github.com/findscripter/everything-skills/tree/HEAD/09-verticals/gene-set-enrichment-analysis) · ★11 · 2 copies)
- **gene-set-enrichment-analysis**: Use when genes must be ranked by epigenomic signal (e.g. RNAP2 or H3K4me3 ChIP-seq prevalence) within chromosome clusters such as BHi-Cect bottom enclaves, and tested for GO-term enrichment via GSEA with Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/gene-set-enrichment-analysis))
- **gene-set-enrichment-analysis**: Use when testing whether a defined gene set (e.g., exercise-responsive DEGs) is enriched for trait/tissue associations in TWAS/S-PrediXcan results across many tissue-by-trait cells, with partial pooling and direction-of-effect testing. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/gene-set-enrichment-analysis--8c24cbf8208ea215))
- **gene-set-enrichment-analysis**: Use when ionomics data (e.g. ICP-MS ion concentrations for haploid S. cerevisiae deletion mutants) have been clustered and you need to identify statistically significant clusters and associated biological processes via GO Slim annotation and GO terms enrichment.
